| `maximhq/bifrost` `docs/openapi/schemas/management/governance.yaml` | `47058e2e` | `Apache-2.0` | `dependentRequired`=1, numeric `exclusiveMaximum`=3 — 75635 bytes | a licence-clean second candidate for two rows that both already have an accepted witness; not screened, and named so a later search does not re-derive it |
| `eclipse-ditto/ditto` `documentation/src/main/resources/openapi/ditto-api-2.yml` | `d75e2629` | `EPL-2.0` | `format: iri-reference`=5 — 519051 bytes | not a witness — publisher-owned (the Eclipse Foundation's own Ditto project) and outside the corpus's redistribution set, and `fern check` exits 1 anyway with `Multiple request properties have the name thingId. This is not suitable for code generation.` Surfaced by the Sourcegraph query only after its rate limit lifted, which is the argument for waiting the limit out rather than recording it as a zero |
| `CumulusNetworks/docs` `static/nmx-api-8513100/openapi.yaml` | `72b5c70b` | `NOASSERTION` | `format: ipv6`=1 — 80390 bytes | not a witness — NVIDIA Cumulus's own NMX API document, outside the redistribution set; not screened, because a second `format-ipv6` candidate blocked on licence changes nothing about the row |
| `dns-technologies/SDMS` `docs/api/sdms-api/openapi.json` | `e0dd5bbe` | `GPL-3.0` | `format: iri`=2 — 154314 bytes | not a witness — outside the redistribution set; recorded because it and Mercure are the only two publisher-owned `format: iri` declarers this search reached that are not Short.io |
| `oasdiff/oasdiff` `data/openapi31/base.yaml` | `a226cecc` | `Apache-2.0` | eleven of these twenty rows in one 3400-byte file — `contains`, `maxContains`, `minContains`, `propertyNames`, `dependentSchemas`, `dependentRequired`, `unevaluatedItems`, `contentMediaType`, `contentSchema`, `$defs` and numeric `exclusiveMaximum`, one declaration each | not a witness — a **synthetic fixture**: it is a diff tool's own test data, which is why one small file happens to declare eleven rarely-used keywords at once. The densest single document this search found, and the clearest illustration of why density is not provenance |
| `QutEcoacoustics/baw-server` `swagger/v2/defs/resources.json` | `9ba186c9` | `Apache-2.0` | `$defs`=1, `maxContains`=1, `minContains`=1 — 4584 bytes | not a witness — a `swagger/v2/defs/` **fragment** rather than a document: it has no `openapi`, `info` or `paths`, so nothing generates from it on its own |
| `spec.openapis.org/oas/3.1/schema/2022-10-07` and `json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/meta/validation` | the published dated schema URLs | the OAI's and the JSON Schema project's own terms | `$defs`=4 and 1, `dependentSchemas`=3, `propertyNames`=1 | not witnesses — **meta-schemas**. They are in the vendor set on purpose, because this region's domain is the 2020-12 dialect, and they are the reason `dependent-schemas` and `dollar-defs` report non-zero vendor counts while neither has a vendor witness |
| SwaggerHub, in bulk | the registry's own `Swagger` property URLs | per document; most declare none | of 7666 distinct public 3.1/3.2 specs, the declaring counts per row are in each row's last cell — 126 for `contentMediaType`, 46 for `propertyNames`, 35 for numeric `exclusiveMaximum`, 33 for `multipleOf`, 16 for `iri-reference`, down to 0 for `dependentSchemas`, `unevaluatedItems`, `idn-hostname` and `relative-json-pointer` | no witness among them. The permissively licensed ones are Petstore 3.1 copies (`frank-kilcommins/Swagger-Petstore-31`), personal re-uploads of somebody else's API (`westriverconsulting/azure-ai-foundry-models-service` re-publishing Azure's, `DJ311ROCKS/open-ai_api` and `ssb81/openai-api` re-publishing OpenAI's) or test schemas (`test-2a8-f7e/qtTestSchema`); and SwaggerHub versions are editable in place, so the registry offers no immutable reference even for the rest |
| `jentic/jentic-public-apis`, in bulk | `eb9d12a2` | repository `CC0-1.0`, per-document otherwise | the shared clone's 6439 `openapi.json` documents walked in full: `multipleOf` 33, `property-names` 12, `format: ipv6` 12, `contentMediaType` 6, numeric `exclusiveMaximum` 6, `format: iri-reference` 3, `contains` 3, `format: iri` 2, `format: json-pointer` 2, `minContains` 2, `contentSchema` 1, `format: idn-hostname` 1, and 0 for the remaining eight rows | no witness among them — the catalog is a third-party aggregation, so every hit is an unproven distribution until the publisher's own copy is found. Its value here is as a pointer: `redirection.io` (31 `iri-reference`), `gcore.com` (19 `ipv6`), `huggingface.co` (53 `propertyNames`), `runzero.com` (14 `ipv6`), `statsig.com` (37 `multipleOf`) and `clickfunnels.com` (10 `contains`) are where a next search should look for a publisher-served original. Two were chased here and neither pans out: `redirection.io` publishes no spec at any URL this search could reach, and `https://api.gcore.com/openapi.json` is live and served but declares none of these twenty rows |

#### The vendor portals this region's domain implies

The bulleted source above says the portals are "the ones this region's own domain implies",
and for these twenty rows that domain is two things at once: the JSON Schema 2020-12
dialect and its registries, which is where the applicator, unevaluated, content and
`$`-keyword rows live; and the network, identity and linked-data APIs whose own fields the
`format-*` rows name — an `ipv6` address, an `idn-hostname`, an `iri`. So the set is 29
documents each publisher serves itself, fetched with `curl -sSL` and walked the same way
every other source's documents were:

- **the dialect's own registries** — `spec.openapis.org/oas/3.1/schema/2022-10-07`,
  `spec.openapis.org/oas/3.1/dialect/base`, `json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema`,
  `json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/meta/validation`, and the OAI's own 3.1 examples
  `OAI/learn.openapis.org` `examples/v3.1/tictactoe.yaml` and
  `examples/v3.1/webhook-example.yaml`;
- **network, infrastructure and data-registry publishers**, where the `format-*` rows'
  own fields live — Cloudflare (both the `openapi.json` and the `openapi.yaml` of
  `cloudflare/api-schemas`), DigitalOcean, the Kubernetes
  `apis__networking.k8s.io__v1` OpenAPI v3 document, NetBox's published 2020-12
  device-type schema, Vercel's `openapi.vercel.sh`, OpenSearch's `_common` 3.1 schema
  document, Sentry, Grafana, Discourse, Crossref's Swagger 2.0 REST API description and
  the OGC's `ogcapi-features-1`;
- **large publisher documents**, where a 2020-12 keyword is likeliest to appear at all —
  OpenAI, Stripe, GitHub, Twilio, SendGrid, Adyen Checkout, Box, Asana, Ory Hydra, Ory
  Kratos and Apicurio Registry.

What they returned is each row's own cell. The set declares five of the twenty at all, and
only three of those five outside a meta-schema: `format: ipv6` (Cloudflare 3, Vercel 1),
`multipleOf` (Cloudflare 8, Box 1, Vercel 1) and `propertyNames` (OpenAI 2, plus 1 in the
OAI meta-schema). The other two, `$defs` (4 in the OAI 3.1 meta-schema, 1 in JSON Schema's
own validation vocabulary) and `dependentSchemas` (3 in the OAI meta-schema), appear
nowhere in the set but a meta-schema. Three further URLs this
set was assembled from could not be read and are not among the 29 — GitLab's
`doc/api/openapi/openapi.yaml` answered `HTTP 429` on the first attempt and `HTTP 404` on
a later one, and the IETF Datatracker's and the RIPE database's advertised
`openapi.json` endpoints both answer `HTTP 404` — and one is fetched but not walked:
`cloudflare/api-schemas`'s YAML spelling, which PyYAML refuses with `could not determine a
constructor for the tag 'tag:yaml.org,2002:value'` at line 460687. That last one costs
nothing, because it is the same document as the JSON spelling that is walked.

#### Every query, shown able to find the shape it looks for

A query that can never match its shape reports zero and reads exactly like a shape nothing
declares. Every one of these twenty queries was therefore run first against something it
must match before its zero was believed about anyone else. Two kinds of thing:

**A control document written for the purpose**, committed beside this file as
[`witness-query-control.yaml`](witness-query-control.yaml). It declares each of the twenty
exactly once, and carries the three decoys the counting rule above has to get right — a
component property literally *named* `contains`, an `example:` block spelling `multipleOf`,
`propertyNames` and `contentMediaType`, and a 3.0 boolean `exclusiveMaximum` beside the 3.1
numeric one. Walked, it yields exactly one of each of the twenty: 1 for `contains` and not
2, 1 for `exclusive-maximum-numeric` and not 2, and nothing at all from the `example:`
block. Both spellings were checked — the YAML file and a JSON rendering of it — because
half these queries are written for one spelling and half for the other. It is a valid 3.1
document in its own right: `fern check` accepts it and `fern generate --group python-sdk
--local --preview` writes a packaged SDK from it, which is the same screen every candidate
faced.

**A real declarer per row**, so that the network queries are shown returning something and
not just the local walk. Each is a document named in the ledger above, and each was found
*by* the query the row records:

| row | the declaration, in the spelling a document writes it | a document the row's own queries do match |
|---|---|---|
| `contains` | `contains:` on an array schema | `grafana/grafana-asserts-public-clients` `openapi.yaml` (2) |
| `content-media-type` | `contentMediaType: application/json` | `ITISFoundation/osparc-simcore` `…/api/v0/openapi.json` (24) |
| `content-schema` | `contentSchema:` beside it | the same oSPARC document (24) |
| `dependent-required` | `dependentRequired:` | `HelixDB/helix-db` `docs/openapi.json` (2) |
| `dependent-schemas` | `dependentSchemas:` | `duy-tung/serving-contracts` `openapi/inference-api.yaml` (1), and the OAI 3.1 meta-schema (3) |
| `dollar-anchor` | `$anchor: Name` | `inkeep/chat-api-openapi-schema` `openapi.yaml` (2), this table's own recorded witness |
| `dollar-defs` | `$defs:` | `emergentmethods/flowdapt` `openapi.json` (7) |
| `exclusive-maximum-numeric` | `exclusiveMaximum: 100` | the oSPARC document (8) |
| `format-idn-email` | `format: idn-email` | `wttw/aboutmyemail` `ameapi.yaml` (4) |
| `format-idn-hostname` | `format: idn-hostname` | Short.io's `https://api.short.io/openapi.json` (1) |
| `format-ipv6` | `format: ipv6` | `linode/linode-api-docs` `openapi.json` (3) |
| `format-iri` | `format: iri` | APIs.guru `mercure.local/0.3.2` (2) |
| `format-iri-reference` | `format: iri-reference` | `stryd/efulfilmentshop-go` `api/openapi.yaml` (36) |
| `format-json-pointer` | `format: json-pointer` | `dcm-project/k8s-container-service-provider` `api/v1alpha1/openapi.yaml` (2) |
| `format-relative-json-pointer` | `format: relative-json-pointer` | `geo-engine/BioIS` `openapi.json` (1), this table's own recorded witness |
| `max-contains` | `maxContains: 4` | `pitekusu/shittim-chest` `contracts/records/v1/openapi.json` (24) |
| `min-contains` | `minContains: 1` | the same Shittim Chest document (24) |
| `multiple-of` | `multipleOf: 0.5` | `cloudflare/api-schemas` `openapi.json` (8) |
| `property-names` | `propertyNames:` | corpus row 109 `volview-backend-contract` (7) |
| `unevaluated-items` | `unevaluatedItems: false` | `grafana/grafana-asserts-public-clients` `openapi.yaml` (2) |

Every row therefore has both: a document written to be found, and a document nobody wrote
for this search that the same query found anyway. `format-relative-json-pointer` is the one
row whose real declarer is the blocked witness this table already recorded rather than
anything the re-search turned up, which is the same statement its outcome makes.

#### Reproducing each source's sweep

The APIs.guru and SwaggerHub sweeps are the two that are not a single command.
Both are plain fetch-and-grep over a public registry index, and both are quoted
above by their result rather than by a script, so here is what to re-run:

- **APIs.guru** — `curl -sS https://api.apis.guru/v2/list.json`, take every
  version's `swaggerUrl`, fetch each (percent-encoding the eight that contain a
  space), and grep each body for `"$anchor"`, `"$comment"`, `"$dynamicAnchor"`,
  `"$dynamicRef"`, `"$vocabulary"`, `relative-json-pointer` and the five
  unquoted YAML spellings. 2529 APIs, 3992 versions, 0 fetch errors, 0 hits on
  3992 of 3992. As a second, independent reading of the same directory,
  `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/APIs-guru/openapi-directory` (HEAD
  `f04b8d0b`, 2026-04-20) and `grep -rlF '<key>' APIs` over its 4138 spec files
  in 701 provider directories: 0 files for every key but `$comment`, whose one
  file is the `example:` value described in that row.
- **SwaggerHub** — page
  `https://api.swaggerhub.com/specs?specification=openapi-3.1.x&limit=100&page=N`
  (and `openapi-3.2.0`) until a page returns fewer than 100 `apis`, take each
  entry's `Swagger` property URL, fetch it and grep the same eleven strings.
  7683 specs, 0 fetch errors, the 10 declaring specs listed in the ledger.

**The twenty ranked `FIXTURE` rows re-ran both of those sweeps and added three more.** The
re-runs differ from the originals in one way that matters: where the first pass grepped for
a string, this one parsed every document and walked it, so its counts are declarations
rather than occurrences.

- **APIs.guru, re-swept whole.** `curl -sS https://api.apis.guru/v2/list.json` → 2529 APIs
  and 3992 versions; every `swaggerUrl` fetched (the eight containing a space re-fetched
  percent-encoded) with **0 fetch errors**; each body parsed and walked. As the second,
  independent reading, `git clone --depth 1
  https://github.com/APIs-guru/openapi-directory` at HEAD `f04b8d0b` (2026-04-20) and the
  same walk over its 4138 spec files in 701 provider directories. The two readings agree
  row for row. Both are a snapshot; re-run them rather than trusting them.
- **SwaggerHub, re-enumerated whole.** Page
  `https://api.swaggerhub.com/specs?specification=openapi-3.1.x&limit=100&page=N` (and
  `openapi-3.2.0`) until a page returns fewer than 100 `apis` — 77 pages plus 1, and a
  stated `totalCount` of 7630 and 67. Taking each entry's `Swagger` property yields
  **7688** URLs, **7666** of them distinct, so the registry lists a handful of entries
  twice and a handful with no spec URL at all. All 7666 fetched and walked; six failed
  first time and all six recovered
  when their owner names were percent-encoded (`Jakub Kępka`, `[GD]hpsk`,
  `Thanyawit_[Palm]` and three others containing a space), so the sweep has no unread
  document in it.
- **Sourcegraph.** `curl -sS -G https://sourcegraph.com/.api/search/stream --data-urlencode
  'q=<query>' -H 'Accept: text/event-stream'`, parsing the `progress` events for
  `matchCount` and the `matches` events for the distinct files. Forty queries, two per row,
  and **all forty answered** — but ten of them only after a long fight with the rate
  limit. The index serves a run of `count:all` queries and then returns `HTTP 429` with a
  Cloudflare `Just a moment…` interstitial for hours; it did that from query thirty
  onwards, which put all ten refusals on the five `format-*` rows purely because they sort
  last. Recovering them took a 90-second cadence, then 3-, 6-, 9- and 12-minute backoffs,
  then a 150-second cadence from an otherwise idle client with no other query in flight,
  over about three hours. Anyone re-running this should pace at one query per two minutes
  from the start rather than discovering the limit at query thirty.
- **The Jentic public-apis catalog**, read from the shared clone at
  `/home/nick/projects/ai-orchestrator/runs/crozier-close-gap-backlog/scratch/163531-4/jentic-public-apis`
  at `eb9d12a2` rather than cloned again — a full clone is about 21 GB and this host had
  under 30 GB free. All **6439** `apis/**/openapi.json` documents parsed and walked. It is
  not one of this region's declared sources and no row rests on it; it is recorded because
  it is the largest single body of real-world OpenAPI this search read, and its hits are
  the pointer list in the ledger's last row.
- **The vendor set**, listed in
  [its own subsection](#the-vendor-portals-this-regions-domain-implies) above: 29 documents
  fetched with `curl -sSL` and walked, with three unreachable URLs recorded there rather
  than silently dropped.

Neither sweep is gated by anything, and both are a snapshot: a registry that
gains a 3.1 document tomorrow is not in the counts above. Re-run them rather
than trusting them, exactly as the [variant scan](#the-supplementary-variant-scan)
asks.

### The annotated-`$ref` pass, and the five gaps it found

The twelve rows at the end of the table are this region's whole share of the
**twelve** selectors the annotated-`$ref` pass declared — ten conjunctions and
two predicates, all of them anchored on a Schema Object. They name one path
through `src/ir.rs`: an `allOf` holding exactly one `$ref` beside members that add
nothing but a description, which is how a 3.0 document attaches documentation to a
shared schema, and the arms that then decide what such a property becomes in
Python. Each row is one census invocation over the whole **169**-source registered
set (32 vendored, 137 fetched):

    just surface-census --selector 'schema.allOf:annotated-ref'
    just surface-census --selector 'schema.properties>schema.allOf:annotated-ref&schema.allOf>schema.$ref~>schema.oneOf'

**Seven of the twelve are `golden` and five are `gap`**, every one of the five
`FIXTURE`. Four of the five came back at zero across all 169 sources, the way the
node-local and pointer-form passes' gaps did. **The fifth did not, and it is the
first row in this file of its kind:**
`annotated-ref-target-composed` is declared **36 times by two registered
sources** — `box.com` (34) and `asana.com` (2) — and neither can ever carry a
committed golden, because both are screened-and-dropped documents Fern's own check
refuses (25 and 17 fatal diagnostics,
[`../../tests/fixtures/AGENTS.md`](../../tests/fixtures/AGENTS.md)). The
classification precedence reads on the golden, not on the declaration, so the row
is `gap`: no byte comparison against Fern touches the branch. What it needs is a
*redistributable document Fern accepts* rather than a plausible one, which is why
its settlement cell says so instead of reciting the usual sibling-spelling
argument.

Corpus row 144, `paloalto-cspm-alerts`, is that document, and rows 144 to 146
witness `annotated-ref-target-oneof` beside it: both rows are `golden` now. Their
evidence cells record that the goldens reach the component builder's
`use_site_copy` rather than the `prop_type_ref` arm each row names.

**Seven of the ten conjunctions descend through the resolving operator, and that
is what these rows measure that no earlier pass could.** `~>` resolves the
annotated reference and the member after it reads a field of the *target*, so the
row for a target declaring `oneOf` counts the referencing node and not the
component's own declaration site — which is already counted, once, where it is
written. The two `hoist_union_variant` rows do not descend: that arm is entered on
the resolution alone and reads no field of the target, so their last member asks
only whether the reference resolves.

**The `oneOf`/`anyOf` split earned its keep here.** The two union-variant rows are
one arm reached through `one_of.as_ref().or(any_of.as_ref())`, and the two halves
came back on opposite sides: the `anyOf` spelling is `golden` on braintrust's
`score_type` query parameter, and the `oneOf` spelling is declared by no
registered source at all. A single selector naming `oneOf` would have reported the
whole arm absent.

**Golden-classified is not golden-exhausted here either**, and each of the twelve
rows says so in its own cell for the same reason every other branch row does.

### What this change's probes settled

The three rows this region still carried as witness-supply probes —
`dollar-dynamic-anchor`, `dollar-dynamic-ref` and `dollar-vocabulary` — are
measured and `limitations`. All three are `discards`: Fern accepts each shape,
exits 0 at both stages, and emits nothing derived from it. The full measurement
is [`fern-limitations.md`'s Round 5](../fern-limitations.md#round-5--the-seven-witness-supply-probes);
what it settled, in one line each:

| key | probe | what Fern did |
|---|---|---|
| `dollar-dynamic-anchor` | [`dollar-dynamic-recursion.yml`](probes/dollar-dynamic-recursion.yml) | `$defs.node` reaches no module, and `StrictTree` collapses to `StrictTree = Tree` with or without the anchor redefinition that is supposed to change what `#node` means inside it |
| `dollar-dynamic-ref` | the same document | `$dynamicRef: "#node"` is not resolved: the array it types emits `typing.Optional[typing.List[typing.Any]]`, where the static control [`dollar-dynamic-recursion-control.yml`](probes/dollar-dynamic-recursion-control.yml) emits `typing.Optional[typing.List["Tree"]]` with `from __future__ import annotations` and `update_forward_refs(Tree)` |
| `dollar-vocabulary` | [`dollar-vocabulary.yml`](probes/dollar-vocabulary.yml) | the schema declaring the map under a custom `jsonSchemaDialect` emits the bare-object default `typing.Dict[str, typing.Any]`, and a schema written against that dialect keeps `count: typing.Optional[int]` though the map declares the 2020-12 validation vocabulary `false` |

**One probe for the dynamic pair, and why they could not be exercised apart.**
`$dynamicRef` is a reference and `$dynamicAnchor` is what it resolves to, so a
document declaring either alone declares a dangling half and measures nothing
about the keyword under test. One document declares both, and both rows are
settled on it.

**Nothing here is a corpus row.** The probe documents are committed under
[`probes/`](probes/) so the measurements can be repeated; none is registered as a
fixture, none sits under `tests/fixtures/`, and the round registered no source —
so this region's census evidence and the search lines above are untouched.
crozier was generated from each probe and byte-matches Fern on all 40 files of
each, which corroborates the verdicts and is counted nowhere: a probe produces no
parity evidence.

**All three stay convertible.** The entry rows name what a registrable witness
would have to be — an OpenAPI 3.1 document a real API publishes as its own
description, declaring the keyword at a pinned credential-free direct spec URL
under a redistribution-compatible licence and accepted by `fern check`. One
turning up promotes the row from `limitations` to `golden` under
[the classification precedence](../openapi-surface-coverage.md#the-category-rules),
which is what `dollar-comment` did as corpus row 109.

### Registration choices for the 30 reconciled keys

The [two committed source reports](witness-search-redo/README.md) reconcile to
**4 `witness-found` and 26 `search-incomplete` outcomes**, with zero `none-found` outcomes. Each row
above records all seven exact query/results. SwaggerHub and Postman are unanswered
for every key; their unanswered status remains recorded but does not override
a retained artifact that passed every screen.
These are search outcomes, not statements that no usable witness exists.
PayPal is now registered as corpus row 138: `anyof-sole-member` is `golden`,
and the other 29 rows remain `gap` / `FIXTURE`. No `limitations` classification
is created; the search outcomes and source answers remain recorded separately
from golden coverage.

Every declaring artifact's four screens are recorded once in the
[candidate dispositions](witness-search-redo/candidates.md), grouped across its
keys. The original shard evidence remains preserved. Screens apply redistribution
permission, immutable publisher provenance, Fern acceptance, then shape retention.
An unfinished Fern check has no refused verdict: Attentive's run ended without an
acceptance result, so its candidate remains `search-incomplete`. A generated model
that allows extra properties does not retain a closed-object item; that candidate
is `witness-blocked` at retention. Neither case is evidence of absence.

The registration successor has these explicit choices, already screened in the
committed reports, in their recorded preferred-document order. Registration
uses that order: PayPal first, followed by Simple JWT Login and Cradl. PayPal
cleared the registration screens, so no lower-listed document replaces it:

| key ready to register | preferred immutable publisher document | four-screen proof |
|---|---|---|
| `anyof-sole-member` | [PayPal catalogue products](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/paypal/paypal-rest-api-specifications/90e8041ffe02d80c452d2b476bedd59a8d219bdc/openapi/catalogs_products_v1.json) | Document/repository redistribution admitted; PayPal owns the pinned repository; CLI 5.118.0 check and generator 5.20.0 both exit 0 (153 files); all four sole-member wrappers reach the concrete FourHundredOne/Three/Four/TwentyTwo details item models. |
| `oneof-bare-object-example-variant` | [Simple JWT Login](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nicumicle/simple-jwt-login/667763ffcff73d326226a771b39155d6be2b1fb9/openapi/openapi.yaml) | Document/repository redistribution admitted; publisher-owned pinned repository; CLI 5.118.0 check and generator 5.20.0 both exit 0 (123 files); AuthBodyPayload and AuthRefreshBodyPayload preserve both string/object unions. |
| `anyof-array-variant-struct-item` | [Cradl publisher description via pinned jentic import](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jentic/jentic-public-apis/eb9d12a2684b0fbcb5aecf51e8ae54dba0929743/apis/openapi/cradl.ai/main/2026-01-28T09:00:46Z/openapi.json) | Redistribution admitted by the source report; immutable credential-free aggregation reference with publisher trace to docs.cradl.ai and equal normalized source/artifact bytes; Fern check and generation pass (232 Python files); GroundTruthList retains two list variants and the GroundTruthListZeroItem structured model. |

The catalogue report additionally records retained candidates for
`ref-pointer-undeclared-component-head` (including Spendesk and Tally). Their
unresolved references become `typing.Any` in the corresponding generated endpoints
or block models. The registration successor can assess those fallback witnesses
against the concrete pointer behavior it intends to cover; they are not needed to
establish the three preferred witnesses above.

#### PayPal registration measurement

Corpus row 138 uses the first preferred document above, at its exact pinned
publisher commit. The publisher's `LICENSE` at that commit supplies the grant;
`NOTICE` retains attribution. The registration journey fetched that raw document,
generated it with Fern CLI 5.67.1 / Python generator 5.20.0, and byte-compared the
unedited, comment-stripped golden against crozier with no exclusions.

The standard surface census reports **4** `schema.anyOf:sole-member` declarations
and **0** for each of the other 29 selectors in the owned-key contract. All four
occur in `components.schemas` 400/401/403/404, at `properties.details.items`.
Their generated evidence is the four `four_hundred*_details_item.py` models named
in the authoritative row and the parent models' typed `details` lists. Thus the
one declared owned key is `golden`; no declared owned key is a Fern discard.
The remaining 29 keys retain their authoritative search outcomes: three are
`witness-found` on other artifacts and 26 are `search-incomplete`. Neither
SwaggerHub nor Postman has been relabelled as answered.

Counts use the standard census, not the local search helper's custom-conjunction
mode, which counts this standalone predicate twice. Preserved search query/results
are historical evidence and remain unchanged.

#### Wide acquisition reconciliation

The [wide acquisition and slot reconciliation](witness-scrape-wide/README.md#finished-tree-reconciliation)
retains all 26 search-incomplete baseline keys. Its ten declaring artifacts yield
no passing witness: eight lack evidenced publisher grants and Fern refuses two.
Slot 1 independently exhausted the empty ranked queue. No registered golden or
category changes, so the region and fixture backlog retain their counts.
The linked report names each key's outstanding search and registration reason.
Postman is excluded from that run and its future obligations; the historical
seven-source query/results above remain unchanged.

### Witness search (exhaustive)

This is the one search record each `gap` row in this file carries under
[Contract B](../openapi-surface-coverage.md#what-makes-a-search-exhaustive): one
line per key, one segment per declared source, and each segment's counts are
the rows that source's `records.tsv` holds for the key, grouped by disposition.
`RankedBacklogTests` reconciles every segment with its ledger and with the
consolidated [`candidates.tsv`](witness-search-github/candidates.tsv). It also
holds each line's outcome to its segments. `exhausted` owes no outstanding
candidate anywhere. `search-incomplete` names, after `outstanding:`, every
declared source still owing one.

The inline `search outcome` text some of these rows' evidence cells still carry
is the frozen
[witness-search-redo contract](witness-search-redo/contract.md)'s seven-source
record. That contract's reconciler reads it verbatim, so it stays as history.
It is not the key's search record and settles nothing. The two sources it names
that Contract B excludes, SwaggerHub and Postman, appear in no line below.

**The redo contract, as amended by the `handwritten-evidence` node.** A row of a
key that contract owns may become `handwritten`, and its evidence cell then
carries no inline history. For such a row the reconciler,
`tools/witness-search/witness-search-redo.py --reconcile`, still requires exactly one row for
the key. It reads the row's `search:` link in place of the history, and requires
that link to resolve to the key's line below and that line to state the verdict
the cell states. The shard records stay reconciled as for every other key, and
the wide scrape's baseline keeps the key exactly when the shards read it
`search-incomplete`. The contract's own files are byte-pinned by
[`historical-sha256.tsv`](witness-scrape-wide/historical-sha256.tsv), so the
amendment is stated here rather than in them.

Five keys read `exhausted`: `annotated-ref-target-string-const`,
`oneof-array-variant-annotated-ref-item`,
`oneof-array-variant-anyof-discriminated-union-item`,
`oneof-array-variant-anyof-nullable-item` and
`oneof-bare-object-example-variant`.
Every candidate the six sources returned for them is decided.
The 4,380 documents the census's standard-library YAML loader refused, across
every key searched here, were read again by the full YAML parser (`just
test-census-fallback` covers the
[re-census script](../../tools/witness-search/witness-search-recensus.py)), and each is
censused or recorded `census-refused` with the parser's error. Of the
candidates whose pinned blob GitHub answered 404 for, six in two deleted forks
were read from the fork's parent, which still holds the same blob. Six keys still
read `search-incomplete` for one reason: 12 `github-code-search` candidates
whose pinned blob GitHub answered 404 for. Each was requested again at its
repository's current revision, at Sourcegraph's mirror of the pinned commit
and in every repository named as its own is, and all three refused it.
[The renewed search](witness-search-renewed/README.md) took routes these
records do not show as tried. It read five of the 12 from the source's own
acquisition cache (`census 0`), and every new route refused the other seven. It
found each of the six keys `none-registrable`, so their rows are `handwritten`.
Their lines below keep the verdict their ledgers give, `search-incomplete`.
`oneof-anyof-variant` and `oneof-closed-empty-object-variant` had their first
search in the remaining-gap searches, and each found the witnesses its row is
now `golden` on.
